I know that probably sounds small, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Has this lack of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty much most days, most of the day. I just don’t really look forward to the things I used to—fishing stuff’s still sitting there, the woodshop feels like work, and even chess with the kids… I’m there, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble enjoying things most days? Also, have you noticed feeling tired or having little energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, I’ve been pretty worn out. By midmorning it can feel like I’ve already put in a full shift, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nEven just walking the project or going up stairs with the crew, my body feels heavy… like I’m carrying wet concrete or something. I’m still trying to push through, but the energy just isn’t there like it should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or that you’re failing your family or yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like admitting that. It sounds dramatic, and I know I probably shouldn’t think that way, but… yes.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m letting everybody down. My wife, the kids… even the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something normal like a missed appointment or a cost overrun, or if there’s a safety issue I have to deal with, I take it really personally. Like it’s proof I shouldn’t be the one in charge.\n\nI know that may not be rational, but in the moment it feels like I’m just… useless. Like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one responsible. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating much less or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make a big thing of it, but food just hasn’t been appealing. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll skip breakfast a lot, then even after being out on a job site all day, I’ll just sort of pick at dinner. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not living up to who you should be almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself, but it’s been pretty much every day.\n\nI keep looking at things and thinking I’ve failed my wife and the kids, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—a missed appointment, a cost issue, a safety problem—I take it as proof that I’m just… not up to it. Like I’m useless, or like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one in charge.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. But that’s where my head goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble thinking clearly or concentrating lately—like it’s harder to make decisions or stay focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have noticed that. I mean, I can still do my job, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it’s definitely harder.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subcontractors, all the moving parts straight in my head. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll start to say something and then just… lose it. Forget what point I was making.\n\nAnd decisions that used to take me five minutes, I’ll just freeze on them now. I keep second-guessing myself. Is that what you mean by concentration?\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy and concentration?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had rough patches before, sure, like anyone with work and family stuff. But I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I didn’t pay enough attention at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. I can’t think of a clear episode where it all lined up this way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can say yes to that clearly. I’ve had times where I was stressed or worn down, but I don’t remember a stretch before where I was down and not interested in things for two weeks like this.\n\nI don’t want to give you a bad answer, but nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life, before this current period, where you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can clearly remember.\n\nI’ve definitely had bad weeks before, stress and feeling run-down, but before this, I can’t point to a time where I was down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more. I hope that’s a useful answer—I just don’t want to overstate it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble thinking clearly or concentrating lately—like it’s harder to make decisions or stay focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have noticed that. I mean, I can still do my job, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it’s definitely harder.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subcontractors, all the moving parts straight in my head. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll start to say something and then just… lose it. Forget what point I was making.\n\nAnd decisions that used to take me five minutes, I’ll just freeze on them now. I keep second-guessing myself. Is that what you mean by concentration?\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy and concentration?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had rough patches before, sure, like anyone with work and family stuff. But I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I didn’t pay enough attention at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. I can’t think of a clear episode where it all lined up this way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can say yes to that clearly. I’ve had times where I was stressed or worn down, but I don’t remember a stretch before where I was down and not interested in things for two weeks like this.\n\nI don’t want to give you a bad answer, but nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life, before this current period, where you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can clearly remember.\n\nI’ve definitely had bad weeks before, stress and feeling run-down, but before this, I can’t point to a time where I was down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more. I hope that’s a useful answer—I just don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this one where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. I mean, I’ve had stressful stretches and times I was tired or not myself, but before this… I can’t remember a solid two weeks where all of that was happening together.\n\nI hope that answers it the right way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just don’t really look forward to the things I used to—fishing stuff’s still sitting there, the woodshop feels like work, and even chess with the kids… I’m there, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble enjoying things most days? Also, have you noticed feeling tired or having little energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, I’ve been pretty worn out. I’m still trying to push through, but the energy just isn’t there like it should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or that you’re failing your family or yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like admitting that. It sounds dramatic, and I know I probably shouldn’t think that way, but… yes.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m letting everybody down. My wife, the kids… even the guys at work. Like it’s proof I shouldn’t be the one in charge.\n\nI know that may not be rational, but in the moment it feels like I’m just… useless. Like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one responsible. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. I just don’t really look forward to the things I used to—fishing stuff’s still sitting there, the woodshop feels like work, and even chess with the kids… I’m there, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble enjoying things most days? Also, have you noticed feeling tired or having little energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, I’ve been pretty worn out. I’m still trying to push through, but the energy just isn’t there like it should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or that you’re failing your family or yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like admitting that. It sounds dramatic, and I know I probably shouldn’t think that way, but… yes.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m letting everybody down. My wife, the kids… even the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something normal like a missed appointment or a cost overrun, or if there’s a safety issue I have to deal with, I take it really personally. Like it’s proof I shouldn’t be the one in charge.\n\nI know that may not be rational, but in the moment it feels like I’m just… useless. Like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one responsible. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not living up to who you should be almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself, but it’s been pretty much every day.\n\nI keep looking at things and thinking I’ve failed my wife and the kids, and the guys at work too. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. I know that probably sounds small, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Has this lack of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty much most days, most of the day. I just don’t really look forward to the things I used to—fishing stuff’s still sitting there, the woodshop feels like work, and even chess with the kids… I’m there, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble enjoying things most days? Also, have you noticed feeling tired or having little energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, I’ve been pretty worn out. By midmorning it can feel like I’ve already put in a full shift, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nEven just walking the project or going up stairs with the crew, my body feels heavy… like I’m carrying wet concrete or something. I’m still trying to push through, but the energy just isn’t there like it should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or that you’re failing your family or yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like admitting that. It sounds dramatic, and I know I probably shouldn’t think that way, but… yes.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m letting everybody down. My wife, the kids… even the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something normal like a missed appointment or a cost overrun, or if there’s a safety issue I have to deal with, I take it really personally. Like it’s proof I shouldn’t be the one in charge.\n\nI know that may not be rational, but in the moment it feels like I’m just… useless. Like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one responsible. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating much less or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make a big thing of it, but food just hasn’t been appealing. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll skip breakfast a lot, then even after being out on a job site all day, I’ll just sort of pick at dinner. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not living up to who you should be almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself, but it’s been pretty much every day.\n\nI keep looking at things and thinking I’ve failed my wife and the kids, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—a missed appointment, a cost issue, a safety problem—I take it as proof that I’m just… not up to it. Like I’m useless, or like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one in charge.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. But that’s where my head goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble thinking clearly or concentrating lately—like it’s harder to make decisions or stay focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have noticed that. I mean, I can still do my job, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it’s definitely harder.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subcontractors, all the moving parts straight in my head. I can’t think of a clear episode where it all lined up this way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can say yes to that clearly. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but I’ve just kind of stopped caring about things I used to look forward to.\n\nLike, my fishing stuff is still sitting in the garage untouched, and my woodshop… it used to be relaxing, but now it just feels like another chore. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. I know that probably sounds small, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Has this lack of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty much most days, most of the day. I just don’t really look forward to the things I used to—fishing stuff’s still sitting there, the woodshop feels like work, and even chess with the kids… I’m there, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble enjoying things most days? Also, have you noticed feeling tired or having little energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, I’ve been pretty worn out. By midmorning it can feel like I’ve already put in a full shift, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nEven just walking the project or going up stairs with the crew, my body feels heavy… like I’m carrying wet concrete or something. I’m still trying to push through, but the energy just isn’t there like it should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or that you’re failing your family or yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like admitting that. It sounds dramatic, and I know I probably shouldn’t think that way, but… yes.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m letting everybody down. My wife, the kids… even the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something normal like a missed appointment or a cost overrun, or if there’s a safety issue I have to deal with, I take it really personally. Like it’s proof I shouldn’t be the one in charge.\n\nI know that may not be rational, but in the moment it feels like I’m just… useless. Like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one responsible. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating much less or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make a big thing of it, but food just hasn’t been appealing. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll skip breakfast a lot, then even after being out on a job site all day, I’ll just sort of pick at dinner. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not living up to who you should be almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself, but it’s been pretty much every day.\n\nI keep looking at things and thinking I’ve failed my wife and the kids, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—a missed appointment, a cost issue, a safety problem—I take it as proof that I’m just… not up to it. Like I’m useless, or like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one in charge.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. But that’s where my head goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble thinking clearly or concentrating lately—like it’s harder to make decisions or stay focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have noticed that. I mean, I can still do my job, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it’s definitely harder.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subcontractors, all the moving parts straight in my head. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll start to say something and then just… lose it. Forget what point I was making.\n\nAnd decisions that used to take me five minutes, I’ll just freeze on them now. I keep second-guessing myself. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. I can’t think of a clear episode where it all lined up this way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can say yes to that clearly. I’ve had times where I was stressed or worn down, but I don’t remember a stretch before where I was down and not interested in things for two weeks like this.\n\nI don’t want to give you a bad answer, but nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life, before this current period, where you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can clearly remember.\n\nI’ve definitely had bad weeks before, stress and feeling run-down, but before this, I can’t point to a time where I was down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more. I hope that’s a useful answer—I just don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this one where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess it’s mainly that I haven’t really been myself. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but I’ve just kind of stopped caring about things I used to look forward to.\n\nLike, my fishing stuff is still sitting in the garage untouched, and my woodshop… it used to be relaxing, but now it just feels like another chore. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. I know that probably sounds small, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Has this lack of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy lasted most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been pretty much most days, most of the day. I just don’t really look forward to the things I used to—fishing stuff’s still sitting there, the woodshop feels like work, and even chess with the kids… I’m there, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having trouble enjoying things most days? Also, have you noticed feeling tired or having little energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, I’ve been pretty worn out. By midmorning it can feel like I’ve already put in a full shift, which is embarrassing to say.\n\nEven just walking the project or going up stairs with the crew, my body feels heavy… like I’m carrying wet concrete or something. I’m still trying to push through, but the energy just isn’t there like it should be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or that you’re failing your family or yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like admitting that. It sounds dramatic, and I know I probably shouldn’t think that way, but… yes.\n\nI keep feeling like I’m letting everybody down. My wife, the kids… even the guys at work. If something goes wrong, even something normal like a missed appointment or a cost overrun, or if there’s a safety issue I have to deal with, I take it really personally. Like it’s proof I shouldn’t be the one in charge.\n\nI know that may not be rational, but in the moment it feels like I’m just… useless. Like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one responsible. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating much less or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make a big thing of it, but food just hasn’t been appealing. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, if that makes sense.\n\nI’ll skip breakfast a lot, then even after being out on a job site all day, I’ll just sort of pick at dinner. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you're not living up to who you should be almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself, but it’s been pretty much every day.\n\nI keep looking at things and thinking I’ve failed my wife and the kids, and the guys at work too. If something goes wrong—a missed appointment, a cost issue, a safety problem—I take it as proof that I’m just… not up to it. Like I’m useless, or like everyone would be better off if I wasn’t the one in charge.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. But that’s where my head goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble thinking clearly or concentrating lately—like it’s harder to make decisions or stay focused on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have noticed that. I mean, I can still do my job, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it’s definitely harder.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subcontractors, all the moving parts straight in my head. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll start to say something and then just… lose it. Forget what point I was making.\n\nAnd decisions that used to take me five minutes, I’ll just freeze on them now. I keep second-guessing myself. Is that what you mean by concentration?\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy and concentration?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had rough patches before, sure, like anyone with work and family stuff. But I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I didn’t pay enough attention at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. I can’t think of a clear episode where it all lined up this way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I’m not sure I can say yes to that clearly. I’ve had times where I was stressed or worn down, but I don’t remember a stretch before where I was down and not interested in things for two weeks like this.\n\nI don’t want to give you a bad answer, but nothing specific comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life, before this current period, where you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can clearly remember.\n\nI’ve definitely had bad weeks before, stress and feeling run-down, but before this, I can’t point to a time where I was down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more. I hope that’s a useful answer—I just don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this one where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. I mean, I’ve had stressful stretches and times I was tired or not myself, but before this… I can’t remember a solid two weeks where all of that was happening together.\n\nI hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt down, lost interest in things, and had trouble with energy or concentration for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can still do my job, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but it’s definitely harder.\n\nI used to be able to keep schedules, subcontractors, all the moving parts straight in my head.
